I searched for episode contests and I didn't find one. I think this could be fun! So, it's sorta simple: make episodes with themes in it! I don't mean one theme for the entire episode, I mean exactly this.

00-0 Simple
00-1 Outside the house
00-2 Machinery(inside them, too)
00-3 Made up place (example: chunky chip land)
00-4 Anything to do with sports

Only I will be judging.

Rules

1. There must be 5 levels, hence the name Episodes with Themes.
2.Must post to NUMA with a link on this topic.
3.Only post to NUMA and here if you're done making all five levels.
4. If you have made episodes or levels that match to these themes, you can re-submit. tktktk, let's just hope you don't give simple challenges to us on 00-0.
5.Call them episode zero.

The episodes will be judged on this....

Total: 30 points
Tileset: 5 points
Originality: 10 points
Fun: 15 points

Judging starts at June 1.

I will answer questions now.

Sweep's is very similar to a frame-by-frame animation. To do one, turn on onion skinning and then the line tool. Draw a line, the press F7. You're on the next frame. Continue until you're done. Thanks for the tip shark. I just wanted to let a little bit of my frustration out. It helps to post.

And to Wolfgang: sniping is caused by 2 things:

Jealousy:

Jealousy is if a mapmaker knows they can't be as good as another good mapmaker, so they try to make us stoop down to their level by sniping.

Revenge:

Revenge, where you rate and comment and say for example, "The map could use a little more work. The Z snap gets to everyone". Then you rate a 2 because that's what you really think. The snipers take it the wrong way, so they snipe you for revenge.

I have good advice: a characteristic of a good map is how the objects just seem to "grow" around the tileset. The gameplay of the map must be how the tileset is meant to be. For example: if you make an air-based map, put in objects that would seem good and fun in air. The bounceblock with one mine under it is a classic.

#11290 1 2006-09-17T22:51:05Z Zres
New idea. How about 3 people make seperate parts of a sig, then we mix 'em together. I'll need people to work with different sigs in different sections:

Group 1:

1:Base Image-Zres
2:Add-ons-Chasethebase
3.Render-Nemetacyst

Group 2

1:Base Image- Shark
2:Add-ons-Borealis
3: Render-ngarner

Group 3

1:Base Image-Jakel
2:Add-ons-zey_
3: Render-elaxion

A person starts with the base image. The add-on person will make the base image look nicer by adding things to it (duh). The renderer will find a render he/she likes, and adds it to the sig.

If you wish to be in this, read this carefully:

When someone finishes the base image, he/she will PM the image to the add-on person. That person PMs it to the renderer when he/she's finished. When the renderer puts in a render, he/she PMs it back to the judge.

Judge:Homestar Runner

So, who's in?

And you may NOT post your image to the thread. When you're done, PM it to Homestar Runner.

You may start imaging now.

Have fun!

#1 Style: Pick the type of image you want. You have three choices.

Abstract:

Expected time to finish: 10 minutes.

Modern:

Expected time to finish: 25 minutes.

Auravuay (My Style)

Expected time to finish: 10 minutes.

#2 Color: Choose the color the image is based on.

#3 Render (optional): Pick the object in the sig.

#4 Font: Choose the font.

So, hopefully your order will come to your house finished.

There is a list where you wait your turn. If five people fill up the list, the next person will have to wait for a place to be empty.

Also when N is in boss mode it only takes a fraction of the comptime it usually takes. Sometimes I can't even look through folders when N is up because the comp's so slow. Then I just enter boss mode and voila, problem solved.

#12440 1 2006-11-24T18:01:41Z Zres
I'm not sure if this is right, but I think that there should be tutorials to let newbies know how to make decent maps. Therefore, I made a tutorial to show people how to make a map similar to my latest map, Lemongrand. Well, here it goes.


Lemongrand is a nice, metanet-esque level. It's simple to make a map like that.

1. I start with an empty map, then I make a jumble of E-tiles.

Simple? Good.

2. I patch up the E-tiles with 1 tiles. You can patch them up with 2's,6's,3's and 7's if you like.

Still easy? I hope so.

3.This is when it gets a little more complicated. I put stand-alone E-tiles in what we just created, a room.


4. I connect these tiles with mines. You don't have to connect all of them.

I hope you're following this.

5. To some, drones are hard to put in, mostly because of their paths. You can usually find a good path for drones. I put in zap drones here. All the drone's paths are CCW Dumb.

6. You find a good spot for the exit and it's switch. Put N near the exit.


7. Just put in gold wherever you want and feel free to put in extra things to make it more challenging.


And we're done! Sorry if this confuses you. I'm completely aware that the tutorial's vague. And I didn't save the level so there's no end result for you.

Well, I hope you can do good with this!

Quote: apg, November 27, 2006 08:36 am
By the way, what IS the logic behind that "top rated" -list? I mean, why does my "Graffiti" -N art map change its position daily? I don't get it...

You see, the top-rated maps could actually be rated something like 4.9. But of course, it still shows 4.5., whilst your graffiti could be rated something like 4.6 and it would still show 4.5.

Summary: Some maps may seem to have the same rating, but there are little decimal points that you can't see that make the rating different.

What?

For you people that have stuck around, you're probably thinking, "Again?". Well this time, I'll make sure everything is in order, and it won't die like the same thread back in October '06. Remember? That Group Sig thread?

How?

Anyway, here's how I'll do it.

If you're one of the people from back then, you should remember how it goes. Three people will group up as a team and will make an image. But unlike last time, there won't be a special duty for each member in a group. You all make part of an image and it doesn't matter what you put in it.

Who?

Simple: Three judges, one back-up judge, and five teams. The first one to join a team will be the team leader. The team leader gets to pick the team name, and can make a logo if they want to (the logo can't be over 100x100 pixels).

When?

The first round will start when all of the spaces are filled in.

NINJAS:
Real ninjas: Ninjas wear black hooded outfits and use large amounts of kung-fu, sword-fighting, and ninja stars. They are often held up by wires and fight to restore their family honor.

Crappy Naruto ninjas: Wear colors, teleport, shape-shift, and use large amounts of bad jokes and nerdy-ness. They often are acting like idiots and moving their lips so they don't match their words.
First, real ninjas.

You're using a lot of stereotype there. They use large amounts of what? Wow, a lot of ninja stars. Not true; they usually only have one or two in their pockets for emergencies like battles. Same with the sword fighting and the kung-fu. Ninjas try to be non-violent and sneak through something to take back what's rightfully theirs *coughNcough*.

Now, Naruto.

If you bothered to think about what they were saying, you would know that they wear colors, teleport, and shape shift because they are in a ninja academy and are students. The bad, nerdy jokes and idiotic behavior only goes to Naruto, the main character. And their lip-syncing doesn't match up because it is originally a Japanese show. Us Americans have to voice over the original sounds so everyone else in America that happens to like the show *coughnotyoucough* can understand it.

Now, don't get me started on One Piece because I'll be all over you.

GET IT!?

#330403 15 2007-06-12T14:14:15Z Zres
OK, that map you gave me:

The tileset is almost fine, but the ninja's placement shouldn't be in a one-tile hole. It's pointless. Try to make holes like that atmost five spaces. Some of the tiles don't seem to flow together, if you know what I mean. Also, Usaswim is kind of right. Looks do make some of the map.

Now, the objects. Nothing much is wrong here but the looks. The only things that you put half-way into a wall are mines. Also, build the locked doors in a place like a tunnel, or a small gush in the wall.

That's all I can tell you. But read this: There really isn't an instant teaching process that lets you make good maps instantly. I learned to make a good map by going into a state of solitary confinement from NUMA and practicing. What are you going to do?

#430178 2 2008-04-20T12:29:43Z Zres
Zer0, please understand that making a game isn't that simple. You need to learn a programming language, and start from there. Some examples of languages are C++, Java, Actionsript, etc. It might take you a very long time to learn any of those, and might cost you some money doing so. Even Game Maker needs you to understand everything about it to make a game in a few weeks or so.

Once you learn a programming language, you have to start with the bare bones of the game (movement, physics, the basic concept of the game). It will take you a while to write that down in the language. Once you're done with that, you can add in levels, sprites, and animations into the game, while going over the programming code again*.

After you've done all of that, congrats. You have just made a basic game. Emphasis on basic. I have no idea how making an online game works, so sorry. Learning how to make games takes much patience and willingness to learn; a lot of people go to school for making games. So, in conclusion, if you want to make a game, make sure you absolutely want to. You need the patience.

*I'm not sure if this is correct. Someone tell me if it's not.
